Simple though many of uHsOfMorEget it. EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT #43 Kavya Away Associate Trader Those who never made mistakes, never tried! Posts: 493 Thank you received: 589 Price always gives aggressive and big moves in direction of trend. When I started my trading career bear trend was established and going on so every short move was giving aggressive and big momentum. But when I learnt most about the market, by that time market changed it's trend from bear to bull , but as my psych was made that market gives aggressive moves down I was unable to get profit out of this bull market. Posts: 493 Thank you received: 589 Hey guys , just a small lesson on how to trade candle PA! Infact, candlesticks are great to find out the Kinks. But which candle we should look for ? I personally like to look for Swing High Candle or Swing Low Candle. Break of Swing High candle is like breaking some kind of RS in LTF which might exist on left. But if CAPS forms near SHC (Swing High Candle) or SLC (Swing Low Candle) that is even great as it is FTB to MPL! https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2356-homeoffinisher?start=50 34/46 7/27/2018 HomeOfFinisher - Page 3 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT But if we have LS below or nearby to SHC (or SLC) then the low of that candle again becomes important RS Flip. But if there is QM on LS with just 2 candles then that QM is also need to pay attention. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2356-homeoffinisher?start=50 35/46 7/27/2018 HomeOfFinisher - Page 3 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Yes , MPL's are great placed to trade for FO when they are just near to Kink or what my friend Sali named ORFL. If you look for every candle, it means less but when you look for SHC and SLC along with PA and Kink it will definitely help you out to take to the pip entries! What inspired me to look for candle PA? RTM's old article which is now locked kept ditching my mind every time that IF started candle PA means there must be something there to look for candle. But then many time I lost looking for old terms all over internet. But then suddenly I got surprised when I was looking for engulf of candle. Then I got my eureka moment and sharp bounce to next level of trading. Posts: 493 Thank you received: 589 If the signal is given by the market mover (an engulf into a new zone), then price is very unlikely to break the extreme of the zone it just came from. So, if you have the patience to wait at the last remaining set of unfilled orders in the origin, you can be almost certain that, that price will be respected. Look for tricks being played on approach to a place where a trick was played, and you'll find where price will turn. If you can look beyond S/D, you'll see that the Flag Limit is the only true place to look for PA for a trade. Trade these levels and you're taking gambling out of your method. All we can ever hope for is a bounce at these levels, unless we are certain that this level is the deepest possible point of retrace after a previous engulf. Without that, we must watch PA as it develops, both before and during the trade. Set and Forget trades can only be taken once the signal is given and the target is clear. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2356-homeoffinisher?start=100 21/36 7/27/2018 HomeOfFinisher - Page 5 - RTM Community Has price tested the last flag on approach? Posts: 493 Thank you received: 589 Trend lines -- How trend lines are best intrument for ITs to trap retail traders? Most of the (I must say all of the) retail traders look for trend lines as potential supply demand areas! as they find price sticking to trend lines herd get confidence in putting there orders at trend lines. And as soon as they see break to those trend lines (but less to see engulf of zone) they tries to trade opposite (breakout orders) at nearest supply demand areas or support resistance areas! But when bunch of retail orders coming into market ITs have really great chance to have big profit against those orders. They won't trade immediate zones(LTF zones) in most of the cases ,but they will setup good looking Higher High's or Lower Low's trend formation {again on smaller scales (this might be Weekly w.r.t. Monthly)}. )So what's the point we should bear in mind in this case? Whenever price closes near trend line , it indicates that trend is slowing down. So we can look for potential opportunity to go against those orders! Orders might be sitting on Monthly , where price failed to reach or clean orders immediate to Swing Highs or https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2356-homeoffinisher?start=100 32/36 7/27/2018 HomeOfFinisher - Page 5 - RTM Community Lows which could setup traps for herds as well as for fund managers! Posts: 493 Thank you received: 589 Hey guys , just a small lesson on how to trade candle PA! Infact, candlesticks are great to find out the Kinks. But which candle we should look for ? I personally like to look for Swing High Candle or Swing Low Candle. Break of Swing High candle is like breaking some kind of RS in LTF which might exist on left. But if CAPS forms near SHC (Swing High Candle) or SLC (Swing Low Candle) that is even great as it is FTB to MPL! https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2356-homeoffinisher?start=50 34/46 7/27/2018 HomeOfFinisher - Page 3 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT But if we have LS below or nearby to SHC (or SLC) then the low of that candle again becomes important RS Flip. But if there is QM on LS with just 2 candles then that QM is also need to pay attention. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2356-homeoffinisher?start=50 35/46 7/27/2018 HomeOfFinisher - Page 3 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Yes , MPL's are great placed to trade for FO when they are just near to Kink or what my friend Sali named ORFL. If you look for every candle, it means less but when you look for SHC and SLC along with PA and Kink it will definitely help you out to take to the pip entries! What inspired me to look for candle PA? RTM's old article which is now locked kept ditching my mind every time that IF started candle PA means there must be something there to look for candle. But then many time I lost looking for old terms all over internet. But then suddenly I got surprised when I was looking for engulf of candle. Then I got my eureka moment and sharp bounce to next level of trading. Posts: 493 Thank you received: 589 If the signal is given by the market mover (an engulf into a new zone), then price is very unlikely to break the extreme of the zone it just came from. So, if you have the patience to wait at the last remaining set of unfilled orders in the origin, you can be almost certain that, that price will be respected. Look for tricks being played on approach to a place where a trick was played, and you'll find where price will turn. If you can look beyond S/D, you'll see that the Flag Limit is the only true place to look for PA for a trade. Trade these levels and you're taking gambling out of your method. All we can ever hope for is a bounce at these levels, unless we are certain that this level is the deepest possible point of retrace after a previous engulf. Without that, we must watch PA as it develops, both before and during the trade. Set and Forget trades can only be taken once the signal is given and the target is clear. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2356-homeoffinisher?start=100 21/36 7/27/2018 HomeOfFinisher - Page 5 - RTM Community Has price tested the last flag on approach? Attachments: Pred!ct0r QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU Treding The Trend Lines! #124 https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2356-homeoffinisher?start=100 31/36 7/27/2018 HomeOfFinisher - Page 5 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Kavya Away Associate Trader Those who never made mistakes, never tried! Posts: 493 Thank you received: 589 Trend lines -- How trend lines are best intrument for ITs to trap retail traders? Most of the (I must say all of the) retail traders look for trend lines as potential supply demand areas! as they find price sticking to trend lines herd get confidence in putting there orders at trend lines. And as soon as they see break to those trend lines (but less to see engulf of zone) they tries to trade opposite (breakout orders) at nearest supply demand areas or support resistance areas! But when bunch of retail orders coming into market ITs have really great chance to have big profit against those orders. They won't trade immediate zones(LTF zones) in most of the cases ,but they will setup good looking Higher High's or Lower Low's trend formation {again on smaller scales (this might be Weekly w.r.t. Monthly)}. )So what's the point we should bear in mind in this case? Whenever price closes near trend line , it indicates that trend is slowing down. So we can look for potential opportunity to go against those orders! Orders might be sitting on Monthly , where price failed to reach or clean orders immediate to Swing Highs or https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2356-homeoffinisher?start=100 32/36 7/27/2018 HomeOfFinisher - Page 5 - RTM Community Lows which could setup traps for herds as well as for fund managers! Posts: 493 Thank you received: 589 Hey guys , just a small lesson on how to trade candle PA! Infact, candlesticks are great to find out the Kinks. But which candle we should look for ? I personally like to look for Swing High Candle or Swing Low Candle. Break of Swing High candle is like breaking some kind of RS in LTF which might exist on left. But if CAPS forms near SHC (Swing High Candle) or SLC (Swing Low Candle) that is even great as it is FTB to MPL! https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2356-homeoffinisher?start=50 34/46 7/27/2018 HomeOfFinisher - Page 3 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT But if we have LS below or nearby to SHC (or SLC) then the low of that candle again becomes important RS Flip. But if there is QM on LS with just 2 candles then that QM is also need to pay attention. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2356-homeoffinisher?start=50 35/46 7/27/2018 HomeOfFinisher - Page 3 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Yes , MPL's are great placed to trade for FO when they are just near to Kink or what my friend Sali named ORFL. If you look for every candle, it means less but when you look for SHC and SLC along with PA and Kink it will definitely help you out to take to the pip entries! What inspired me to look for candle PA? RTM's old article which is now locked kept ditching my mind every time that IF started candle PA means there must be something there to look for candle. But then many time I lost looking for old terms all over internet. But then suddenly I got surprised when I was looking for engulf of candle. Then I got my eureka moment and sharp bounce to next level of trading. Posts: 493 Thank you received: 589 If the signal is given by the market mover (an engulf into a new zone), then price is very unlikely to break the extreme of the zone it just came from. So, if you have the patience to wait at the last remaining set of unfilled orders in the origin, you can be almost certain that, that price will be respected. Look for tricks being played on approach to a place where a trick was played, and you'll find where price will turn. If you can look beyond S/D, you'll see that the Flag Limit is the only true place to look for PA for a trade. Trade these levels and you're taking gambling out of your method. All we can ever hope for is a bounce at these levels, unless we are certain that this level is the deepest possible point of retrace after a previous engulf. Without that, we must watch PA as it develops, both before and during the trade. Set and Forget trades can only be taken once the signal is given and the target is clear. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2356-homeoffinisher?start=100 21/36 7/27/2018 HomeOfFinisher - Page 5 - RTM Community Has price tested the last flag on approach? Posts: 493 Thank you received: 589 Trend lines -- How trend lines are best intrument for ITs to trap retail traders? Most of the (I must say all of the) retail traders look for trend lines as potential supply demand areas! as they find price sticking to trend lines herd get confidence in putting there orders at trend lines. And as soon as they see break to those trend lines (but less to see engulf of zone) they tries to trade opposite (breakout orders) at nearest supply demand areas or support resistance areas! But when bunch of retail orders coming into market ITs have really great chance to have big profit against those orders. They won't trade immediate zones(LTF zones) in most of the cases ,but they will setup good looking Higher High's or Lower Low's trend formation {again on smaller scales (this might be Weekly w.r.t. Monthly)}. )So what's the point we should bear in mind in this case? Whenever price closes near trend line , it indicates that trend is slowing down. So we can look for potential opportunity to go against those orders! Orders might be sitting on Monthly , where price failed to reach or clean orders immediate to Swing Highs or https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2356-homeoffinisher?start=100 32/36 7/27/2018 HomeOfFinisher - Page 5 - RTM Community Lows which could setup traps for herds as well as for fund managers!
